# Genesis Mission — Master Reference Coverage Report

> **Document Overview:**
> This report provides a comprehensive coverage analysis, breakdown, and master audit index for the **716 external reference links** compiled within `references.md` for the **Genesis Mission** documentation repository.

such as the one announced by **Google's DeepMind group in 2022**", with the **Variable Configuration Tokamak (TCV), Lausanne** and the **DIII-D tokamak at General Atomics, San Diego** named as hoped test beds; ***Liquid Metal Alloys*** (**David Hatch / Narayana Aluru / Yuanyue Liu / Md Rashidul Alam**) — **lithium, indium, gallium or tin** liquid-metal plasma-facing components, "No pure liquid metal has all the properties that are desirable for a fusion reactor", **tens-of-picoseconds** alloy–plasma simulations training ML models for "much longer timescales … using far fewer computing resources", the concepts "first developed at UT Austin" having "led to a spinoff company … called **ExoFusion**", test partners **Penn State** and Princeton's soon-to-be-online **NSTX-U**; ***Plugging Magnetic Bottles*** (**Josh Burby / Nicholas Nelsen / Matthew Meeker**) — alpha-particle-orbit surrogate "perhaps **10 to 100 times as fast**" than the state-of-the-art model, the page's **only** performance figure and expressly a hope; ***Living on the Edge*** (**Del-Castillo-Negrete / George Biros / Joseph Wick**) — faster, cheaper **divertor / scrape-off-layer (SOL)** simulation via "numerical techniques that are new, at least in this setting". **Surrogate pattern**: "**three of the four** newly funded collaborations involve taking big, complex computer simulations … and **replacing them with far more efficient AI models that have been trained on the data produced by their beefier predecessors**"; **workforce development** for the commercial fusion industry is the second stated goal (del-Castillo-Negrete: the students "get Ph.D.s related to this work, publish papers and gain experience that will carry them on into industry or academia"). **Sector framing**:
